1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
6	\label{sec:exp-setup}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	\subsection{Main Results}
16	
17	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\ref{app:subpopulation}).

Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\,BH adj.\ $p = 0.042$; $^{3s}$\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate; $^\dagger$\,4-seed replication in Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed} (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). 55	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp despite $\kappa \in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.
56	
57	\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\pm$2\,pp corresponds to ${\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\sigma = 2.70$\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\pm$2\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\,pp, so $\pm$2\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\pm$2\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\pm$1\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\pm$3\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (MDE $\leq 2$\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\Delta| \leq 4.41$\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\Delta = +0.92 \pm 1.14$\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}12\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.pdf}\n\\caption{Causal DAG of the self-extraction pipeline. Traces $T$ produce both answers $A$ and constraints $C$; under degeneration (Observation~1), $C$ is determined by $A$ (dashed arrow), yielding $I(C;Y) \\leq I(A;Y)$.}\n\\label{fig:causal-dag}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}), making weight proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the DPI \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}), revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for NLI. Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does, confirming structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\\ref{app:subpopulation}).\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). Two conditions show significant degradation after BH correction ($m{=}6$ confirmatory; remaining exploratory); no condition shows a significant gain. $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,BH adj.\\ $p = 0.042$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate; $^\\dagger$\\,4-seed replication in Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed} (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\\%$ for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\\%$: QwQ-32B AIME$^\\ddagger$ $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K$^\\ddagger$ $n{=}30$) yield $\\Delta = 0$ and are reported separately in Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}. $^\\ddagger$Pilot conditions ($n{\\leq}30$); included for coverage, not confirmatory evidence. Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B$^\\dagger$ & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp despite $\\kappa \\in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\\pm$2\\,pp corresponds to ${\\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\\sigma = 2.70$\\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\\pm$2\\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\\,pp, so $\\pm$2\\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\\pm$2\\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\\pm$1\\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\\pm$3\\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (MDE $\\leq 2$\\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\\Delta = +0.92 \\pm 1.14$\\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).\n\n",

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Self-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning via majority voting over chain-of-thought (CoT) traces.
3	A natural extension is to extract formal constraints and aggregate them with a symbolic solver; but does it work?
4	Under zero-shot extraction, we test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT.
5	Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1{,}200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence tests ($\pm 2$\,pp margin); the remaining 7 are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\,pp) but show consistent directional evidence ($|\Delta| \leq 4.41$\,pp).
6	We diagnose this as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models up to frontier scale.
7	The mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size $K_\text{eff}$ from inter-trace agreement $\kappa$: correlated traces collapse nominal $K{=}12$ to $K_\text{eff}$ as low as 1.20.
8	Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck.
9	As preliminary evidence (exploratory; $n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs), a cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), suggesting that architectural independence can restore corrective signal.
10	Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure modes from a pilot run\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026/symb_invariant_consensus}.}.
11	\end{abstract}
12	
```

A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver—but we show this is a \\emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. [Director 指令] **重写 Abstract**

将 abstract.tex 替换为以下内容（保留 SICA 名称，其余按新叙事逻辑重写）：

```latex
\begin{abstract}
Self-Consistency (SC) improves LLM reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver—but we show this is a \emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck, revealing a fundamental independence--accuracy trade-off: methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal, suggesting that the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.
\end{abstract}
```

**关键点：**
1. 第 3 句介绍 SICA（保留方法名 + MAX-SAT）
2. 删除所有统计检验细节（TOST/MDE/FDR/p-value/条件数/K_eff）
3. 只保留 2 个数字：9 models、BR 1.66-4.97×
4. 脚注格式和匿名链接不变
5. 确认 ≤ 200 words

### Tool result

_source line 1876 · 2026-05-25T11:12:45.856Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01A7JxGFgYHyHvZ8qJqSQzjt`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Self-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning via majority voting over chain-of-thought (CoT) traces.
3	A natural extension is to extract formal constraints and aggregate them with a symbolic solver; but does it work?
4	Under zero-shot extraction, we test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT.
5	Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1{,}200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence tests ($\pm 2$\,pp margin); the remaining 7 are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\,pp) but show consistent directional evidence ($|\Delta| \leq 4.41$\,pp).
6	We diagnose this as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models up to frontier scale.
7	The mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size $K_\text{eff}$ from inter-trace agreement $\kappa$: correlated traces collapse nominal $K{=}12$ to $K_\text{eff}$ as low as 1.20.
8	Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck.
9	As preliminary evidence (exploratory; $n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs), a cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), suggesting that architectural independence can restore corrective signal.
10	Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure modes from a pilot run\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.
11	\end{abstract}
12	
```

    "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-Consistency (SC) improves LLM reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver—but we show this is a \\emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck, revealing a fundamental independence--accuracy trade-off: methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal, suggesting that the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

          "-A natural extension is to extract formal constraints and aggregate them with a symbolic solver; but does it work?",
          "-Under zero-shot extraction, we test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT.",
          "-Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1{,}200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence tests ($\\pm 2$\\,pp margin); the remaining 7 are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but show consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp).",
          "-We diagnose this as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models up to frontier scale.",
          "-The mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size $K_\\text{eff}$ from inter-trace agreement $\\kappa$: correlated traces collapse nominal $K{=}12$ to $K_\\text{eff}$ as low as 1.20.",

    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning via majority voting over chain-of-thought (CoT) traces.\nA natural extension is to extract formal constraints and aggregate them with a symbolic solver; but does it work?\nUnder zero-shot extraction, we test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1{,}200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence tests ($\\pm 2$\\,pp margin); the remaining 7 are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but show consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp).\nWe diagnose this as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models up to frontier scale.\nThe mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size $K_\\text{eff}$ from inter-trace agreement $\\kappa$: correlated traces collapse nominal $K{=}12$ to $K_\\text{eff}$ as low as 1.20.\nTwenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck.\nAs preliminary evidence (exploratory; $n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs), a cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), suggesting that architectural independence can restore corrective signal.\nThree zero-cost diagnostics predict failure modes from a pilot run\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
    "userModified": false
  },

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
6	\label{sec:exp-setup}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	\subsection{Main Results}
16	
17	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\ref{app:subpopulation}).

62	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp despite $\kappa \in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.
63	
64	\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\pm$2\,pp corresponds to ${\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\sigma = 2.70$\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\pm$2\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\,pp, so $\pm$2\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\pm$2\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\pm$1\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\pm$3\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (MDE $\leq 2$\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\Delta| \leq 4.41$\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\Delta = +0.92 \pm 1.14$\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}12\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).

We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\\ref{app:subpopulation}).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.pdf}\n\\caption{Causal DAG of the self-extraction pipeline. Traces $T$ produce both answers $A$ and constraints $C$; under degeneration (Observation~1), $C$ is determined by $A$ (dashed arrow), yielding $I(C;Y) \\leq I(A;Y)$.}\n\\label{fig:causal-dag}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). Two conditions show significant degradation after BH correction ($m{=}6$ confirmatory; remaining exploratory); no condition shows a significant gain. $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,BH adj.\\ $p = 0.042$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate; $^\\dagger$\\,4-seed replication in Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed} (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\\%$ for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\\%$: QwQ-32B AIME$^\\ddagger$ $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K$^\\ddagger$ $n{=}30$) yield $\\Delta = 0$ and are reported separately in Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}. $^\\ddagger$Pilot conditions ($n{\\leq}30$); included for coverage, not confirmatory evidence. Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B$^\\dagger$ & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp despite $\\kappa \\in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\\pm$2\\,pp corresponds to ${\\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\\sigma = 2.70$\\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\\pm$2\\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\\,pp, so $\\pm$2\\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\\pm$2\\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\\pm$1\\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\\pm$3\\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (MDE $\\leq 2$\\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\\Delta = +0.92 \\pm 1.14$\\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).\n\n",

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Self-Consistency (SC) improves LLM reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver—but we show this is a \emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck, revealing a fundamental independence--accuracy trade-off: methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal, suggesting that the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.
3	\end{abstract}
4

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces, as in neuro-symbolic approaches \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}, and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\begin{figure*}[t!]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}
9	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
10	\label{fig:pipeline}
11	\end{figure*}
12	
13	\looseness=-1
14	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.
15	
16	\looseness=-1
17	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.
18	
19	\looseness=-1
20	Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\S\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence, a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
21	
22	Our contributions are:
23	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
24	\item \textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \S\ref{sec:remediation}).
25	
26	\item \textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
27	
28	\item \textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence ($n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs) that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
29	\end{enumerate}
30	
31	
```

```text
1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\geq}99\%$ of problems (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
8	
9	\smallskip\noindent
10	\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
11	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
12	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
13	
14	\smallskip\noindent
15	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
16	Z3 rejection rate $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}|/|\mathcal{C}| \approx 0.034$ confirms $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\ref{app:yield-cv}), making weight proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
17	
18	\label{sec:process-kappa}
19	\looseness=-1 The $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the DPI \citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\, Y) \leq I(A;\, Y)$ (Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\ref{app:cil}).
20	
21	\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}
22	\label{sec:remediation}
23	\label{sec:interventions}
24	
25	\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}), revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\kappa = 0.15$, $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\kappa$ (${\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}).
26	
27	\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}
28	\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}
29	\label{sec:diagnostics}
30	
31	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.
32	
33	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\Delta = -4.41$\,pp) and non-significance ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).
34	
35	\begin{figure}[t]
36	\centering
37	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}
38	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}
39	\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}
40	\end{figure}
41	
42	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}
43	\label{sec:cross-arch}
44	
45	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli} for NLI. Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.
46	
47	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
48	
49	\begin{table}[t]
50	\centering
51	\small
52	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}
53	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
54	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
55	\toprule
56	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
57	\midrule
58	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
59	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.665 \\
60	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.503 \\
61	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.453 \\
62	\bottomrule
63	\end{tabular}}
64	\end{table}
65	
66	\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\% (Appendix~\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does, confirming structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\ref{app:drc}).
67

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WsQcN7EDuapYhqaAerGxVj`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	\looseness=-1
5	Formal verification's value depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. SICA reduces to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B to frontier). Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure from a $K{=}12$ pilot (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). Cross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), suggesting \emph{architectural diversity} over scale. Trained verifiers---PRM \citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}---succeed by breaking the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ chain with external signal; our NLI escape instantiates this without task-specific training ($+6.67$\,pp). Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
6	
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	\label{sec:pipeline}
4	\label{sec:condorcet}
5	\label{sec:effective-k}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), the effective ensemble size $K_\text{eff}$ is
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
11	\end{equation}
12	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.
13	
14	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	
16	\begin{figure*}[t]
17	\centering
18	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{f2_regime_scatter_v2}
19	\caption{SICA $\Delta$ (pp) vs.\ Fleiss' $\kappa$ (inter-trace agreement). Color encodes model family; shape encodes dataset (\scalebox{0.7}{$\bullet$}\,PW, \scalebox{0.7}{$\blacktriangle$}\,FOLIO, \scalebox{0.7}{$\blacksquare$}\,LogiQA). Error bars show multi-seed standard deviation. High $\kappa$ ($K_{\text{eff}} \to 1$) clusters near $\Delta \approx 0$; only the low-$\kappa$ region admits nonzero effects, consistent with the independence bottleneck.}
20	\label{fig:regime-scatter}
21	\end{figure*}
22	
23	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
24	
25	\looseness=-1
26	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
27	
28	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
29	\begin{equation}
30	\label{eq:maxsat}
31	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
32	\end{equation}
33	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout (Algorithm~\ref{alg:maxsat}; Appendix~\ref{app:z3-pseudocode}). Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
34	
35	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
36	\begin{equation}
37	\label{eq:score}
38	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
39	\end{equation}
40	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
41	
42	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
43	\begin{equation}
44	\label{eq:degenerate}
45	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
46	\end{equation}
47	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
48	
49	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\pm}0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in [0, 1]$).
50	
51	\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \emph{bias ratio} (BR):
52	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mirage}
53	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
54	\end{equation}
55	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1): values above 1 reflect the trace-count base rate inherent to source-attributed constraints. $\text{BR} = 1$ indicates no coupling beyond chance. We validate this metric empirically in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.
56	
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
  "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver, but we show this is a \\emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck; a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges where methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Among training-free approaches, only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal; the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n"
}
```

SICA reduces to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B to frontier). Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure from a $K{=}12$ pilot (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nFor the broader neuro-symbolic community, our results expose a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines: when the same model generates both reasoning and formal constraints, the symbolic layer inherits the model's distributional prior rather than providing an independent check. For test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}, our findings imply that allocating additional inference budget to self-consistent sampling yields diminishing returns once $K_\\text{eff}$ saturates; redirecting compute toward structurally independent verification offers a more efficient path.     "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver, but we show this is a \\emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck; a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges where methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Among training-free approaches, only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal; the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",

  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "3abe9f80-4b59-40ed-9c05-d4afcf9e6825",

    "oldString": ", providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.",
    "newString": "; we provide a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces, as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}, and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence, a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence ($n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs) that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n",

Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.",

Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote.     "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces, as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}, and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings; we provide a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence, a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence ($n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs) that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n",
