    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}), making weight proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the DPI \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}), revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for NLI. Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does, confirming structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}), making weight proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the DPI \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}), revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for NLI. Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does, confirming structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the data processing inequality (DPI) \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}), revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for NLI. Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for NLI. Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does, confirming structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the data processing inequality (DPI) \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does, confirming structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\\ref{app:subpopulation}).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.pdf}\n\\caption{Causal DAG of the self-extraction pipeline. Two conditions show significant degradation after BH correction ($m{=}6$ confirmatory; remaining exploratory); no condition shows a significant gain. $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\\%$ for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B$^\\dagger$ & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp despite $\\kappa \\in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\\pm$2\\,pp corresponds to ${\\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\\sigma = 2.70$\\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\\pm$2\\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\\,pp, so $\\pm$2\\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\\pm$2\\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\\pm$1\\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\\pm$3\\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (MDE $\\leq 2$\\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\\Delta = +0.92 \\pm 1.14$\\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).\n\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}), the effective ensemble size $K_\\text{eff}$ is\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = K \\,/\\, [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa]\n\\end{equation}\n(following \\citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\\text{eff} \\approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{f2_regime_scatter_v2}\n\\caption{SICA $\\Delta$ (pp) vs.\\ Fleiss' $\\kappa$ (inter-trace agreement). Color encodes model family; shape encodes dataset (\\scalebox{0.7}{$\\bullet$}\\,PW, \\scalebox{0.7}{$\\blacktriangle$}\\,FOLIO, \\scalebox{0.7}{$\\blacksquare$}\\,LogiQA). Error bars show multi-seed standard deviation. High $\\kappa$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\to 1$) clusters near $\\Delta \\approx 0$; only the low-$\\kappa$ region admits nonzero effects, consistent with the independence bottleneck.}\n\\label{fig:regime-scatter}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout (Algorithm~\\ref{alg:maxsat}; Appendix~\\ref{app:z3-pseudocode}). Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \\emph{bias ratio} (BR):\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1): values above 1 reflect the trace-count base rate inherent to source-attributed constraints. $\\text{BR} = 1$ indicates no coupling beyond chance. We validate this metric empirically in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.\n",

  "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\\looseness=-1\nFormal verification's value depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. SICA reduces to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B to frontier). Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure from a $K{=}12$ pilot (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nFor the broader neuro-symbolic community, our results expose a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines: when the same model generates both reasoning and formal constraints, the symbolic layer inherits the model's distributional prior rather than providing an independent check. This limitation is not specific to logical constraint extraction; it applies to any self-verification paradigm where the verifier shares the generator's knowledge boundary \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nCross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); this points to \\emph{architectural diversity} over scale as the key design principle. Trained verifiers---PRM \\citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}---succeed precisely because they break the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ chain with independently supervised signal; our NLI escape instantiates this principle without task-specific training ($+6.67$\\,pp). For test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}, our findings imply that allocating additional inference budget to self-consistent sampling yields diminishing returns once $K_\\text{eff}$ saturates; redirecting compute toward structurally independent verification offers a more efficient path. Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n"
}

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
6	\label{sec:exp-setup}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	\subsection{Main Results}
16	
17	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions); constraint-based reweighting thus introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\ref{app:subpopulation}).
18	
19	\begin{figure*}[t]
20	\centering
21	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.pdf}
22	\caption{Causal DAG of the self-extraction pipeline. Traces $T$ produce both answers $A$ and constraints $C$; under degeneration (Observation~1), $C$ is determined by $A$ (dashed arrow), yielding $I(C;Y) \leq I(A;Y)$.}
23	\label{fig:causal-dag}
24	\end{figure*}
25	
26	\begin{table*}[t]
27	\centering
28	\small
29	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). Two conditions show significant degradation after BH correction ($m{=}6$ confirmatory; remaining exploratory); no condition shows a significant gain. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\,BH adj.\ $p = 0.042$; $^{3s}$\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate; $^\dagger$\,4-seed replication in Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed} (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\%$ for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\%$: QwQ-32B AIME$^\ddagger$ $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K$^\ddagger$ $n{=}30$) yield $\Delta = 0$ and are reported separately in Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}. $^\ddagger$Pilot conditions ($n{\leq}30$); included for coverage, not confirmatory evidence. Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}
30	\label{tab:main_results}
```

55	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp despite $\kappa \in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.
56	
57	\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\pm$2\,pp corresponds to ${\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\sigma = 2.70$\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\pm$2\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\,pp, so $\pm$2\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\pm$2\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\pm$1\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\pm$3\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (minimum detectable effect (MDE) $\leq 2$\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\Delta| \leq 4.41$\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\Delta = +0.92 \pm 1.14$\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}12\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions); constraint-based reweighting thus introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\\ref{app:subpopulation}).\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). Two conditions show significant degradation after BH correction ($m{=}6$ confirmatory; remaining exploratory); no condition shows a significant gain. $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,BH adj.\\ $p = 0.042$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate; $^\\dagger$\\,4-seed replication in Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed} (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\\%$ for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\\%$: QwQ-32B AIME$^\\ddagger$ $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K$^\\ddagger$ $n{=}30$) yield $\\Delta = 0$ and are reported separately in Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}. $^\\ddagger$Pilot conditions ($n{\\leq}30$); included for coverage, not confirmatory evidence. Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B$^\\dagger$ & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp despite $\\kappa \\in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\\pm$2\\,pp corresponds to ${\\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\\sigma = 2.70$\\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\\pm$2\\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\\,pp, so $\\pm$2\\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\\pm$2\\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\\pm$1\\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\\pm$3\\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (minimum detectable effect (MDE) $\\leq 2$\\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\\Delta = +0.92 \\pm 1.14$\\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).\n\n",

[Director 指令] **新增 Diagnostics Flowchart + 全文 display items 分散排版**

两个任务：

### 任务 A：制作 §5.3 Diagnostics 的 decision flowchart（Figure 5）

用 TikZ 制作一个 decision flowchart，展示 3 个零成本诊断指标的使用流程：

```
输入: K=12 pilot run
    ↓
[C₀ < 5%?] → Yes → "Degenerate: SICA ≡ SC, skip"
    ↓ No
[K_eff ≤ 1.30?] → Yes → "Low diversity: no corrective signal"
    ↓ No
[BR > 1 + SC regime?]
    → Type A (SC ≥ 60%): "Confirmation bias: SICA degrades"
    → Type C (SC ≤ 40%): "Error amplification"
    → Intermediate: "Potential gain (verify with held-out)"
```

设计要求：
- 单栏宽度 `figure[t]`，`width=\columnwidth`
- 节点用圆角矩形（判断）和矩形（结论）
- 配色与论文其他图一致（蓝/灰色系）
- Caption 自包含，说明这是 practitioners 的 a priori 诊断流程
- Label: `\label{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}`
- 放在 analysis.tex 的 §5.3 Diagnostic Framework 段落之后

### 任务 B：全文 display items 分散排版

目标：**除第 1 页外，每一页都有至少一个 figure 或 table**。

当前 7 个 display items（加上新 Figure 5 共 7 个）：
- Figure 1 (pipeline): p2 ✓
- Figure 2 (regime scatter): p4 ✓
- Table 1 (main results): p6
- Figure 3 (causal DAG): ?
- Figure 4 (kappa-accuracy): ?
- Figure 5 (diagnostic flowchart): 新增
- Table 2 (cross-generator NLI): ?

目标分布（理想状态，尽量接近）：
- p1: 无（只有 title/abstract）
- p2: Figure 1 ✓
- p3: 需要一个 display item（目前是纯文本 Related Work + Method）
- p4: Figure 2 ✓
- p5: 需要一个 display item
- p6: 一个 display item
- p7: 一个 display item

策略：
1. 调整 float 定义位置来控制落页
2. Table 1 是 `table*` 双栏宽，体积大，可以锚定某一页
3. Figure 3 和 Figure 5 都是单栏，可以灵活放置
4. 如果某页实在放不下，可以考虑把 Figure 4 改成 `figure*` 全宽
5. 反复编译微调直到分布满意

**编号约束**：Figure 编号顺序必须和文中首次引用顺序一致。

### 编译验证
- 每页（p2-p7/p8）都有至少一个 display item
- 正文 ≤ 8 页
- 0 undefined reference
- Figure/Table 编号顺序正确

完成后汇报每个 display item 的最终页数。

```text
1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\geq}99\%$ of problems (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
8	
9	\smallskip\noindent
10	\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
11	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
12	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
13	
14	\smallskip\noindent
15	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
16	Z3 rejection rate $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}|/|\mathcal{C}| \approx 0.034$ confirms $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
17	
18	\label{sec:process-kappa}
19	\looseness=-1 The $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the data processing inequality (DPI) \citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\, Y) \leq I(A;\, Y)$ (Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\ref{app:cil}).
20	
21	\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}
22	\label{sec:remediation}
23	\label{sec:interventions}
24	
25	\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\kappa = 0.15$, $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\kappa$ (${\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}).
26	
27	\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}
28	\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}
29	\label{sec:diagnostics}
30	
31	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.
32	
33	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\Delta = -4.41$\,pp) and non-significance ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).
34	
35	\begin{figure}[t]
36	\centering
37	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}
38	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}
39	\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}
40	\end{figure}
41	
42	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}
43	\label{sec:cross-arch}
44	
45	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.
46	
47	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.
48	
49	\begin{table}[t]
50	\centering
51	\small
52	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}
53	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
54	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
55	\toprule
56	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
57	\midrule
58	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
59	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.665 \\
60	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.503 \\
61	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.453 \\
62	\bottomrule
63	\end{tabular}}
64	\end{table}
65	
66	\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\% (Appendix~\ref{app:finetuned-nli}).       "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain provides information-theoretic motivation via the data processing inequality (DPI) \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration, $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does; structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",
